  what the evidence supports.

● Intermediate child task — round 2's Phase-1 literature survey (part of the
  claim/experiment work), not the experiment stage completing. It's useful
  validation of the round-2 approach, and I'll surface two takeaways relevant to
  the experiment stage:

  1. Second predictor → ColabFold/AF2 (downloads cleanly; OmegaFold's torch pin
  was the round-1 blocker), and ESMFold≈AF2 agree on bulk α/β fractions so the
  dual-predictor agreement test is passable — confirms the fallback I flagged to
  the experiment agent.
  2. pLDDT is itself confounded — AlphaFold gives higher pLDDT to de-novo
  proteins even when more disordered, and pLDDT propensity varies by SS class.
  So a helix-raising intervention could move pLDDT and bias a naive readout —
  which is exactly why the plan's P1 (pLDDT-weighted primary + covariate
  conditioning + threshold sweep) rather than a naive gate is the right call.
  The survey confirms confidence-weighted SS statistics is a genuine unfilled
  gap — i.e., round 2's methodological contribution is real, not busywork.

  Timeline reality check for you

  Round-2 M2 is running cleanly (4-wide, all GPUs busy), but I now have a firm
  pace datapoint worth surfacing: the first M2 cell took ~81 min. Each cell
  folds ~300 proteins through both predictors, and OmegaFold (the independent
  second predictor) is several × slower than ESMFold. Across the remaining ~150
  dual-predictor cells (30 M2 + 72 M3 + ~48 null), that's a ~1.5–3 day
  wall-clock — not "into tomorrow." (That first cell likely includes cold-start
  model-load; warm cells should be a bit faster — I'll firm up the ETA after a
  few more.)

  This is the genuine cost of the rigor you asked for — a second independent
  predictor, ~300 samples/dose for tight CIs, a wide σ_proj sweep, and a
  ≥48-direction null. None of it is wasted; it's what makes the causal claim
  publication-solid. But it does cost days.

  Optional lever (your call — non-blocking, it runs either way):

  Option: Let it run as-is (default)
  Result: most rigorous, ~1.5–3 days
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Option: Speed ~2–3×
  Result: OmegaFold defaults to many refinement "recycles"; for a coarse 
  secondary-structure readout (helix fraction) 1–2 recycles is plenty. Dropping
   it ~halves fold time with negligible impact on helix/sheet fractions. Say
  the word and I'll have the agent switch OmegaFold to low-recycle mode for the
   remaining cells (ESMFold unchanged) — a safe speed/precision trade for this
  specific readout.

  I'll keep it running as-is unless you tell me otherwise.
